The main section of the preaching is now over, The two Mantras of this chapter coming further on, tell about the importance of this practice, there the verse portion ends. The next two chapters are in prose form.

The fourth Mantra tells, that the fruit of this practice is the release from the prison of life:

*pratibodha viditam matam-amrtatvam hi vindate*

*atmana vindate viryam vidyaya vindatemrtam \|\|4\|\|*

(What I have learnt by churning mind and reflecting, you too understand it. The one knower of it gains the state of deathlessness. Gets strength by practicing. Gets salvation, by the understanding)

This Mantra also is in Anustup meter.

But in its first line there is an excess of one syllable. Instead of 8, It has 9 syllables: 9+8+8+8=33.

Therefore it is Bhurik Anustup.

Its presiding Deities and other details are as in the previous ones. There is a different reading of it.

The reading opted by the monk Sri Rangaramanuja, who belongs to the tradition of Acarya Ramanjua, is:

*pratibodha viditam amrtam, amrtatvam hi vindate*

In this reading, the first line has two syllables in excess: 10+8+8+8=34.So it becomes Svaradanustup.

But among Srivaisnavas also this reading is not generally accepted. No other commentators have accepted this reading.

The reading accepted by all commentators of the tradition of Acarya Sankara and Acarya Madhva is: pratibodha viditam matam, with 9 syllables. Others have not accepted the reading with ten syllables: pratibodha-viditam amrtam.

Most important is the reading of Trivikrama Pandita, who had studied Upanisat directly from the Acarya. That reading of his tradition, becomes the deciding one to us.

We got that reading from Traivikramaryadasa. Therefore there is no room for confusion here.

He refers, to the reading of only nine syllables; pratibodha viditam matam.

Caturmukha tells Siva: “I reflected continuously on this Narayana Principle. Contemplated, listening to the scriptures and churning the logic. I understood by contemplating. By understanding I saw. You too try to understand It. By trying, see It.”

Matam = churned by contemplating. Viditam = understood. (You too, who got guidance from me, similarly). Pratibodha = understand it.

All the commentators, with the supposition that ‘Pratibodha + Viditam’ is a compound word, have explained the meaning of the Mantra.

But, ‘Pratibodha’ is a different word. To consider it to be a verb, is a finer explanation:

*maya viditam tvamapi pratibodha*

As I have seen you too understand it. only Traivikramaryadasa has shown the clue to this lovely meaning.

‘Pratibodha’ is a form of root ‘budha’.

It is not a noun; it is a verb: budha avagamane.

‘Bodha’ means understanding.

To say, you understand, it was enough if said: bodha viditam.

But here to the word ‘Bodha’ a preposition ‘Prati’ has been added: Prati + Bodha.

Why this ‘Prati’? What does Pratibodha mean?

Bodha is different and Pratibodha is different.

It has several facets.

Let us open it up one by one.

Pratibodha means, waking up from sleep.

Kathopanisat proclaims: uttisthata jagrata; Get up, wake up.

Similarly here also: Pratibodha = wake up for that Brahmatattva. We wake up every dawn from sleep.

Why should Upanisat wake us up?

It is a very strange sleep.

A sleep, which doesn’t wake up while being awake also.

What does it mean?

There is a remark in Mandukopanisat:

*svapna-nidra-yutavadyau, prajnastva-svapna-nidraya*

Its sense is like this:

Awake means a mixture of dream and sleep. The dream also means the same.

Only sleep exists in sleep.

Nowhere is wakefulness, Even in the waking stage wakefulness is not there; If it is not there in dream, not in sleep too.

While standing beyond wakefulness, dream and sleep, there is no dream, nor does sleep... .Only awareness remains.

What does it mean, sleep is there in sleep also?

A very strange riddle.

If the shell of the riddle is broken, one can find a tasty kernel inside.

Scholars call it ‘Nidra’ - sleep; they call it ‘Nisa’ - darkness.

Sleep means not sleep; night means not night.

Ignorance is sleep; Ignorance is verily darkness.

*ya nisa sarvabhutanam...*

Sleep means, not being aware of anything. That verily is Ajnana ignorance.

Dream means false knowledge; one thing seen as something else. In wakefulness, false knowledge is there; Ignorance also is there. Therefore, in wakefulness, dream is there, sleep also is there. Similarly, in dream also.

Watching the dream, we get illusioned that the dream is wakefulness.

Only after we wake up, we realise that it was not waking stage, merely a dream.

Therefore, in the dream, both wakefulness and sleep are there.

Only in sleep, one thing is not seen as another thing. It is a state of seeing nothing.

Therefore, in sleep, only ignorance remains; only sleep remains.

Therefore, sleep means mere sleep.

Waking and dream means, to have sleep and dream.

We should cross all these three. That is the fourth state.

They called it ‘Turiya’ - the fourth one. That is the state ofDhyana. That verily is the state of wakefulness.

Upanisat explains this: pratibodha.

You reach the state of Dhyana. Really wake up.

Then, you will see with your inner-eye, what you have heard, what you have understood by reflection.

In sleep, wakefulness was not there.

In dream also, we didn’t wake up.

In wakefulness also we didn’t wake up:

We fancied that we woke up. But, we lived blind folding our eyes; lived blind like Gandhari.

In these three states, the eye we had, was not useful.

Seers of Upanisat alerted us about this:

*avidyayam - antare vartamanah …*

There are some, who sitting firm, erecting a fence of ignorance, think they are safe within it. Those who go living on, believing their blindfold to be spectacles. Those living carefree by putting Dhrtarastra on the throne! what can be their fate:

*andhenaiva niyamana yathandhah*

Blind people moving guided by another blind!

We have to watch how they reach their destination.

Into which ditch they would fall down, is not known.

The way, the Rsis of Vedic times described the danger, that would befall, if the ignorant scholars guide the path of spirituality, is very fantastic.

It is not just a primeval vision; it is a prime vision.

It is not just an Eastern vision, it is the truth seen by Western practitioners too.

The same remark can be seen in Thomas gospel also. The essence is the same, though the language is different: ‘If a blind man follows another blind man, both of them will fall into a pit’.

The awareness of Truth is uniform all over.

But it appears wearing different tops in different places.

We should not remain stuck with the same garment top. Our attention should be tuned towards the ‘Top’ within the top.

Otherwise there is no end to our blindness.

Here, ‘Pratibodha’ has one more meaning:

It is the wisdom that each person can gain according to his capability.

Each should be awake. But everybody’s wakefulness is not uniform.

There are bowls in thousand types. All are full. But their fullness is not uniform.

All practitioners are filled with god’s awareness. But the amount of the awareness is not uniform among them.

The practice of each one is different. Achievement too is different.

According to each one’s measure, the awareness is gained.

Everything is complete. Yet, everything is incomplete.

Brahma’s awareness is complete; Siva’s awareness also is complete. But that completeness is different and this completeness is different.

According to each ones capacity, each gains his completeness.

When the bowl of each practitioner gets full, amrtatvam hi vindate.

Amrtatvam = the state of deathlessness, Vindate = (he) gains, Hi= It is well known.

‘Hi’ suggests all kinds of fame. This is known in Sastras; Known in the experience of wise ones. It is accepted by all unanimously.

‘Hi’ is a kind of consoling.

Vindate hi = he gains, isn’t it so? It is a known thing to you also.

Isn’t it so? Should I tell it again?

Amrtatva is a state of deathlessness.

Anyone born has to die; jatasya hi dhruvo mrtyuh.

Not to die one should not get born. Therefore the state of not dying is a state of not being born also.

Therefore, Amrtatva means, a state of broken bond of birth and death.

The state of Salvation.

Which is the way to move towards this Salvation? What are the preparations needed for it? The later half of the Mantra provides an answer to this query:

*atmana vindate viryam...*

‘Virya’ means, the inner strength to win over the spiritual empire.

That is very much the foundation of Virya: the job of giving visible form to the invisible.

The Virya of the mind is like the Virya - semen, of the body.

When it is filled up, the secrets not seen so far in the Veda- Mantras emerge, taking forms in front of our eyes and start dancing.

This virya is the very power, which lifts the lid of the case of vedas.

It has to be gained from ’Atma’: atmana vindate viryam...

It looks like an answer. But is it an answer?

From the answer another question emerged again.

What does it mean?

Through which can we gain this inner power? We didn’t succeed despite all our efforts.

Bhagavadgita tells the same:

*manusyanam sahasresu kascid yatati siddhaye*

Someone in thousands tries to proceed in this path.

Yes; the path of spirituality is like that:

*ksurasya dhara nisita duratyaya*

It is a walk on the blade of a sword.

We should not fall down; feet should not be ripped. The very walk is a mysterious art. It is not the path of lotus-eaters. It is the path of the firm ones; the path impenetrable.

*durgam pathastat kavayo vadanti*

Very few keep moving in this path. Among the movers also, very few reach the goal:

*yatatamapi siddhanam kascinman vetti tatvatah*

Among the thousand, one moves ahead, determined. Among such thousand movers one reaches the goal; becomes proficient to understand God properly. All the rest give up at half way and turn back.

Only our ambitious attempt is not enough to reach the target. We need His blessing also.

It is not enough that we yearn to see Him. He too should will to show Himself up.

Therefore, the requirement for this salvation is His compassion.

*atmana vidante viryam*

Atmana = from God, from His blessings, Viryam = the inner power of spirituality, Vindate = gains.

All the Upanisats proclaim this:

*yamevaisa vjtnute tena labhyah*

*tasyaisa atma vivrnute tanum svam*

(Whom He becomes fond of, only to him, He comes into sight; only in front of him, He opens up.)

If we think that with the strength of our intelligence, we can understand God, we will never understand. What is the use of reading so many Sastras? No Sastra opens up in front of us, unless He wills.

If God wills that ‘let this one understand me’, then everything is unveiled. All the lids open out on their own.

Therefore, for the achievement of awareness, God’s blessing is the foremost need: atmana vidante viryam.

To achieve this awareness, another strong qualification needed is, Atmabala - strength of the self. They call it, the prowess of the soul; they call it Anubhava - spiritual progress; They call it Selfefficiency.

For everything, competency is a must; categorically in spiritual practices.

Such a competency should be there in the very existence of the soul itself.

The spiritual practice, done by an unbecoming person may go waste; may turn disastrous too, like the penance of demon Bhasmasura.

Because, to such practice, there is no protection of God’s grace.

God never forgives the practice done beyond one’s competence.

For that reason, the scripture calls Him ‘Edhamanadvit’.

An ant should not grow into the size of an elephant; and an elephant should not shrink down to the size of an ant.

One becoming like another is not progress; it is distortion.

Each should be as it is. That is beauty.

This is the design of God.

Against this background, the word ‘Atma’ opens up with another meaning.

Now ‘Atma’ means, the strength of the self; the capacity of the self.

Atmana = by capability of self, Vindate = gains, Viryam = inner strength.

Thirdly, ‘Atma’ means self-effort.

There is a danger in a superficial sight of spiritualism.

Man may turn lazy, with a pretext of spiritualism.

Those who can’t digest Adhyatma - spiritualism, tell like this: God is the puppeteer of everything. We are mere puppets. What is the use of our efforts? Since He moves everything, let us remain effortless.

This is laziness. Such pessimism sometimes becomes troublesome, wearing a mask of philosophy.

This is misuse of spiritualism; it is a way to downfall.

In Mahabharatatatparyanirnaya, Acarya Madhva presents a very significant message:

*nijanubhava-varjita harer-anugrahojjhitah*

*maha-prayatna-varjita jana na jagmur-unnatim*

(The spiritual competence of Atma, God’s blessing and ambitious attempt, until these three entwine, no one can go high.)

To achieve a thing, one needs competency. The incompetent person, whatever efforts he makes, fails.

Despite having competency, if one doesn’t make efforts, his also fails.

Competency should be present. And also the effort according to one’s competency should be there. Having both these, Yet if god’s grace is missing, both these go waste.

With this background, another new dimension gets added to the word ‘Atma’.

‘Atma’ means self-effort; an effort, firm and unending, till the goal is achieved; a great effort.

Atmana = from the unending effort (one), Vindate = gains, Viryam = inner power.

The three got entwined. Inner-power sprouted.

The door of awareness opened. What next?

*vidyaya vindate amrtam*

Vidya means not mere knowledge.

It is, wisdom through direct perception. It is Aparoksa – Jnana - vision of the invisible; it is Saksatkara - Realization.

Such a person who has gained Realization, he won’t be delayed in gaining Salvation.

Vidyaya = by the perception of god, Amrtam - the deathless, eternal principle, i.e. god. (he), Vindate = gains.

He joins the deathless God; also becomes deathless himself.

Purusasukta also tells the same:

*tamevam vidvan-amrta iha bhavati*

One who perceives Him, to be full of attributes and bereft of all defects, the cause of everything and the regulator of everything, such a person wins over death, here itself.

## Mantra: five

Why should one take up all this Trouble? Without the botheration of this spirituality, why should not one stay carefree?

We don’t want the path taken up by a single one out of a thousand.

The way of the 999 is fine for us.

Even if we want the special path, why should there be haste? Enjoying all the fun and frolic in youth and finally in the old age, if needed, we can go for the rosary.

Some persons, who don’t know what they are talking about, speak thus.

To such ones, the last Mantra of this chapter gives a final warning:

*iha ced-avedidatha satyam-asti*

*na cedihavedinmahat vinastih*

*bhutesu-bhutesu vicitya dhirah*

*pretyasmallocad-amrta bhavanti \|\|5\|\|*

(If we understand here itself, it will be a real success there. If it is not understood here, it will be a great tragedy. The wise ones searching and finding It, as pervading every living being, will escape this mundane world, and will win over death.)

In the two line of the first half of the Mantra, there are 12 syllables each. In the two lines of the later half, there are 11 syllables each. Together it is 46 syllables: 12+12+11 + 11=46. First half is in Jagati meter. Second half is in Tristup meter.

If it is Jagati of 48 syllables, it is Virat Jagati, with two syllables less.

If it is Tristup of 44 syllables it is Swarat Tristup, with two syllables in excess.

Or this can be considered a Vedic sub-category, a mixture of two meters.

It is like the subcategory, a mixture of Indravajra of 11 syllables and Vamsastha of 12 syllables, as we find in the later poetry.

It is like, the rare subcategory, a mixture of Sardulavikridita with 19 syllables and Sragdhara, with 21 syllables, we find in the Nakhastuti of Acarya Madhva.

Tristup having 11 syllables is dear to Ekadasa Rudras. Thus it is dear to Siva, who is reflecting on Brahmatattva with Caturmukha, through this Upanisat.

Jagati, having 12 syllables is dear to the twelve Adityas. Narayana in the form of Vamana is the last one among the twelve Adityas. Therefore, this chandas is dear to Visnu.

Thus, this is a meter related to the inquirer Siva and the inquired Visnu. Thus, with the Chandas of Hari and Íṁrṁ-Visnu and Siva, the main preaching of Caturmukha ends.

The further chapter is in prose.

This Mantra stingingly ridicules those who are delaying taking up the path of knowledge: iha avedit cet...

‘Iha’ means, here.

Here means where?

In these world, in this body, in this life and at this moment.

These many meanings are hid in the womb of ‘iha’.

Don’t postpone the effort of knowing, practice of knowing, to a future life. Start it in this life itself. Who can guarantee that one will be born again as a human being in the coming life?

In this life, doing what should not be done, suffering on the path of misery in that world, and later again if one is born as a tree or a bird or as an animal, how terrible it would be!

Watching the degraded attitudes of man, such a probability seems to be more real.

Let us assume that one is born again as a human being.

Even then, what is the guarantee that again one gets a conducive environment for the practice of knowledge?

It may again be a journey on a deviant path, corrupted by an improper surrounding.

Once the right path is lost, it is very difficult to find the right one in this thick jungle.

Let us assume that one finds proper environs.

Then also, the great laziness within will whisper in the ear: It is okay, If you start practicing in the next life, no need to hurry. Have as much fun in this life.

To block this human attitude, Christianity rejected the concept of rebirth.

The true concept was rejected, but man's bad tendency remained. For that reason, Upanisat warns: iha cedavedit.

Iha = in this very life, Avedit cet = if it is understood, Atha = afterwards, Satyam = the result of real knowing, joy, beauty, success, Asti = is (will be) there.

Through Siva, Caturmukha gives to human being this warning of this message.

Man has to practice in this earth. Other worlds are places of achievements, not the places of practice.

Therefore, don’t lose wastefully the good opportunity of this being born in this Karmabhumi - place for action.

Iha = in this world, in this Karmabhumi, (when you come) Avedit cet = if (you) understand, life will be meaningful.

Or else, a whole life gets wasted.

One can never get it back what is lost.

Is it enough if one comes into this world? To do spiritual practice, one requires a human body.

That is why the ancients said: sariramadyam khalu dharma-sadhanam.

If one fails to practice while he has such human body, there is no certainty that one will get such a chance again.

Even if one gets, the lost can’t be retrieved.

For that Upanisat tells: iha cedavedit...

Iha = when one gets this human body, Avedit cet = it (one) understands life becomes meaningful.

It doesn’t end there.

He is born on earth, born as human being. Born in a surrounding, where he gets the friendship of good practitioners. Yet the same insolence advises him: Don’t go after the stupidity of spiritual search, right now. Don’t get denied of the jovial moments of youth. One day you reach sixty. Then, Vedanta will very much be there. The path of withdrawal comes after retirement. Till then the road of engagement. There is no sense in chasing Dharma and Moksa, buy turning away from Artha and Kama. Don’t be stupid, Enjoy.

Such ones are the ones who are searching pleasures in the path of pain.

Deluded ones under the illusion think that stupidity is cleverness.

For that reason Sruthi warns again: iha cedavedit.

Iha - right at this moment, Avedit cet - if one understood, Atha - later on, Satyam - bliss, the product of truth’s awareness, Asti-is there.

Atha - afterwards means, after what?

After this body crumbles down.

Even after the Revelation, until the body remains, there is no release. Nobody attained Salvation along with the body.

Until the Prarabdha Karmas (the deeds done in previous lives and already started to give results), are fully worn out, even with Revelation there is no release.

When the Karmas are wiped out and this body collapses, only then one attains Salvation.

Geeta tells: jnanagnih sarvakarmani bhasmasat kurute.

The fire of wisdom burns down all the deeds.

Sruti tells: ksiyante casya karmani tasmin drste paravare.

When God, the greatest of all is seen, all the deeds are destroyed. And even after the Aparoksa darsana-Revelation, Prarabdha Karma, remains behind without getting destroyed. What does it mean?

To understand this remark, we should see the two facets of Karma. Our Karmas are of two types:

One started to give the results is called Prarabdha Karma- Potent Karma. Another standing in queue awaits its turn to be fruitful. This is called Sancita Karma-Potential Karma.

Sancita Karmas get fully destroyed.

But the Prarabdha Karmas are to be worn out only by experiencing their effects.

Acarya Sankara gives an example to this:

There are arrows in sheath. One arrow is already harnessed in the bow and shot at the target.

The arrows in the sheath can be shot at the target or just thrown down into a garbage bin.

But the arrow harnessed in the bow and shot at the target, can’t be stopped midway. It has to reach the target and stop.

The situation of the karmas too is similar.

Sancita Karmas are like the arrows in the sheath. They can be discarded without being used.

Prarabdha Karmas are like the arrows moving towards the target. We can’t halt them halfway.

Aparoksajnani, who has seen the Brahman also has to undergo the results of Prarabdha Karma.

After Aparoksa, until Moksa, the time span of wearing out Prarabdha Karma is called the period of Jivanmukti - living Salvation.

The real Moksa - Salvation, is only after going through the Prarabdha Karma.

To point out this topic, the word ‘Atha’ has come in the Mantra: atha satyamasti.

Atha = after the Prarabdha Karma is worn out, Atha = after the final collapse of the body, Satyam = blissful state, Asti= will be gained.

What is the meaning of the word ‘Satyam’?

‘Satyam’ means, God full of all attributes.

‘Satyam’ means, Blissful Salvation.

Bhagavata tells: satyam param dhimahi.

Let us meditate on the great Truth.

Sruti too tells: satyam jnanamanantam brahma.

That is Satya, which is full with positive qualities.

Sat means birth, emergence. The one responsible for it, Creator of everything is Satya.

Sat means, being, sustenance. The one responsible for the sustenance, the caretaker of all, is Satya.

‘Sat’ means Sada, destruction. The one who destroys everything is Satya.

Whatever is ‘Sat’ =exists, Ya= controller of it is Satya; Controller of all.

Sat means knowledge. The one who gives it is Satya.

Sat means Avasada, ignorance. The one who gives it is Satya.

Sat means, the sad bond of worldly life. The one who has kept us in bondage is Satya.

Sat means faultless state, Salvation. By being the giver of Salvation, God is Satya.

The one, who is the cause of the creation, sustenance and dissolution, and the order of the living and non-living, He is satya. The one who gives to the living beings, knowledge and ignorance, bond and Salvation, He is Satya: satyas-samatma sammitas-samah.

God, the cause of all the functions of the universe, is concerned with the Jnanis knowers: satyam asti.

Why only for the knowers? He is for everyone.

For the atheists also. The one who is everywhere, how can He not be there only for some?

Despite his being there for them, it was of no use. Therefore he is not there for some unbelievers.

To be is not to have, to be aware of it is to have.

For that reason Upanisat tells: atha satyamasti.

For such good persons, God is there.

When the other face of this word opens up, ‘Satya’ means Moksa -Salvation.

Lexicographers say:

*sadbhavavacakah sabdah sarve te sakhavacakah*

All the words which tell about being and existence, tell about bliss also.

‘Asti’ means not only ‘he is’, it also means that ‘he is happy’.

Therefore, ‘Sat’ means, being, being happy.

Mukti - Salvation, brings to us such an existence. Therefore ‘Satya’ means Salvation.

Thus, Upanisat gives the assurance: atha satyamasti. To such a person, there is Salvation.

It is alright, if it is understood. If not understood? Or could not be understood? Or if it is postponed that it can be understood later? It will be an irretrievable loss:

*na cedihavedinmahati vinastih*

Iha = in this life, Na avedit cet = if one did not understand, (it is) Mahat = a very big, Vinastih = loss.

There are two reasons for not being understood; hatred and unconcern; antipathy and apathy.

Unconcern or apathy is also of two types; permanent and temporary.

Temporary unconcern is the better of the two. Such people do reach their goal, but belatedly.

It can be explained thus:

We travelled to a town. We should have reached it before evening. Somewhere, we missed our road.

We went about here and there.

When we came across someone on the way, we enquired with him about the right road to our town.

He said; Your town is in the east. You are heading towards west! You have come a long way in the wrong direction.

Then we changed our route again. It became dark by then. We spent the night at somebody’s place. The next morning again we took up the right road and reached our town.

Finally we reached the place. But we lost a day wastefully, losing the right road. The path to God is also similar: If we could find the right path in this life itself, we could have reached faster. But couldn’t find it and had to wander around.

How many lives were wasted! How to compensate this loss? The lost time will never return again.

So the Sruti tells: mahati vinastih.

It is a loss that cannot be made up.

The greater loss even than this is of those who are indifferent for ever.

For them, indifference is a form of life; life-companion; a congenital habit.

It is a ghost - chasing everywhere.

Searching for the road, changing the road from one to another, they keep roaming around. And they are not concerned at all about the right path.

They think like this:

“We are to be born again. We shall practice in the coming life. Let us enjoy the worldly life in this birth.

Why should one lose ones head for the unseen happiness, giving up the one in front of the eye?

Vatsyayana had told:

*varam adya kapota bhaksanam svo mayura-bhaksanat*

Imaging the more delicious peacock meat of tomorrow, why give up the ready cooked pigeon - meat of today and starve?

Hoping that tomorrow we meet the damsels in heaven, why give up enjoying the beautiful girls, ready here and now?”

They don’t realise, what a dangerous argument it is.

They have come believing this argument, in all their hundreds of previous births.

Is there any possibility of their going for the right practice in their coming life? They will have the same argument in the next life also: Let us see in the next time.

There is no remedy to their lethargy.

They will keep circling the foothills, never reaching the peak. These verily are the Nityasamsaris - eternal householders, lasting nomads.

Their loss is greater than that of the first ones.

They reached the goal though after a delay; climbed the hill.

These ones remained where they were, simply saying that they will climb sometime in the future. These will remain ever in the mundane life, without reaching God.

Therefore, the loss of these ones are greater:

*na cedihavedinmahati vinastih*

The third categories are those who hate God. They argue like this:

There is no God; nothing of that sort. What we see, only that is the truth. The pleasure of female body is the real salvation! Enjoy here as much as you can. Don’t be a stupid person, searching after the non-existing happiness, giving up present pleasures.

Such persons always move in the opposite direction to God.

As they move on and on, they go farther and farther away from God. They move to darkness from light.

As they move farther from god, they get closer to darkness. Finally they remain in the dark house for ever.

Krsna tells this in Gita:

*mam-aprapeiva kaunteya tato yanti-adhamam gatim*

(They never reach me; they fall down in the valley, which can never be climbed out of).

It is an endless tragedy.

There is no greater loss than this. A big loss, the final loss.

For that reason, Upanisat warns:

*na cedihavedinmahati vinastih*

We should not spoil our lives like this.

We should start to know God right now.

We have started, understood also.

What further?

*bhutesu-bhutesu vicitya dhirah pretya asmat lokat amrtah bhavanti*

Dhirah = scholars, who have tasted knowledge, Bhutesu-bhutesu = in all living beings, vicitya = searching and realizing (God), Asmat lokat = from this world, from this body, pretya = freed, going up, Bhavanti = become, Amrtah = deathless.

The ones, who searched for God and found Him are ‘Dhiras’, the brave ones. Who have relished the sweetness of knowledge. We have seen earlier, the special meaning of this word: atimucya dhirah pretyasmallokadamrtah bhavanti.

The same utterance of the first chapter has appeared here with a slight change. The fourth line is fully the same: atimucya dhirah pretyasmallokadamrtah bhavanti.

‘Vicitya’ is a word derived from the root ‘Chi sancayane’. ‘Cayana’ means, gathering together, searching out, separating the mixed thing and identifying it.

Presently, it means, searching god and come to know Him after the search.

Where to search? In all living beings: bhutesu-bhutesu.

He pervades each Jiva.

He is inside you, and inside everybody: yadasya tvam yadasya devesu.

That form of God within the Jiva, should be separated from Jiva and be identified. It should be worshipped after identifying; It should be realized through worship.

This topic was dealt in detail earlier: nedam yadidamupasate.

In other places too this reference is there:

*ghrtamiva payasi nisaktam*

*bhute-bhute ca vasati vijnanam*

*satatam manthayitavyam*

*manasa manthana-bhutena*

God is residing in each life, like butter in milk. To see it separately it has to be churned. A continuous churning is needed; a churning of the scriptures, with the churning stick of mind; a churning of the ocean of heart, with the churning rod of God-reflection. Then we discover the butter. That is God.

*payasyupahitam ghrtam svayamiva sriyo vallabham*

Kathopanisat tells the same:

*tvam svat sarirat pravrhet*

The God inside the Jiva, He should be separated from Jiva - Jiva who is like the body of the God- and seen.

Otherwise, there is the danger of Jiva, getting into the illusion that T am the God’, and remain at the level of ‘Soham’ -I am that.

Such persons, failing to see the Tattva - the Principle, beyond them- selves, get into the danger of living a life of self deception.

That is the reason, the Sruti alerts us all the time:

First, you realise the Jiva - the self. By that, the Reflection is understood.

Then emerges the deep desire of witnessing the origin - the God.

By that, you understand God, who is pervading inside the Jiva, and is in the form of the reflected, i.e. the original Image:

*bhutesu bhutesu vicitya*

This topic appears in detail, in Antaryami Brahmana of Brhadaranyakopanisat:

*yah prthivyam tisthan … yopsu tisthan... yognau tisthan… yo vayau tisthan… ya akase tisthan… yah sarvesu bhutesu tisthan… yah prane tisthan… yo vaci tisthan… yascakusi tisthan… yah srotre tisthan… yo manasi tisthan… yo vijnane tisthan… vijnanadantaro yam vijnanam na veda yasya vijnanam sariram yo vijnanamantaro yamayatyesa ta atmantaryamyamrtah*

He is in Pancabhutas - Five elements, He is also in five sense- organs: Is all over the universe: is inside the material world: is inside the Life -world. He, the hidden inside, should be searched out.

By this search, we should escape from this body, from this mundane world and from this life.

*asmat lokat pretya*

Loka means this earth and also this body. The wise ones, thus escaping this bond; amrtah bhavanti, become deathless.

They never again get trapped in this prison of birth and death, this mundane life. By delaying to gain this awareness, which is instrumental for such a great gain, one should not make life wasteful and barren. With this warning, this second chapter of five Mantras ends.

## Section: Three

## Prose: one

In the next two chapters we have a story. A story complementary to the issues raised earlier.

It is not enough, knowing the Deities of the sense-organs. The supreme power, who gives them that power should be known, it was told.

Now, this is a story supporting that; a story of the defeat of those Sense-Deities, at the hands of God.

The following is a tradition of the Upanisats; Telling about a situation and justifying it through a narrative, that it happened so. Its purpose is to bring confidence about a Tattva - Principle, through a narrative of history.

While telling a story, the move of the Upanisat changes from verse into prose, a move that is appropriate to tell the story and also is charmful.

*brahma ha devebhyo vijigye, tasya ha brahmano vijaye*

*deva amahiyanta, ta aiksantasmakamevayam*

*vijayosmakamevayam mahimeti \|\|1\|\|*

(God defeated the Asuras for the sake of Devas. Devas became proud by that victor of the God. They imagined: ‘this is very much our victory, it is very much our own glory’)

It seems, once it happened like this:

Suras and Asuras - Divine and Devilish forces, fought with each other. It was a fight between virtue and wickedness,

Both were children of sage Kasyapa; elder and younger brothers.

Brothers hating each others.

In the Puranas, there are references about the Deva and Danava war a number of times. It is an unending fight between Divine and Evil powers, in heaven.

There were occasions when the Devas were defeated.

In the fight between Bali, the king of Asuras and the Devas, it happened that way.

Devas were defeated; Bali, the king of Asuras won and occupied Indra’s throne.

Similarly, a number of times, Asuras too were defeated.

Isn’t it similar in our lives too?

Some times virtue wins; sometimes evil.

In this story of the Upanisat, Devas won and Asuras were defeated.

But Upanisat tells: In reality, it was not the Devas, who were victorious. God desired, let the Devas win. And staying within the Devas, for the sake of Devas, He defeated the Asuras: brahma ha devebhyo vuigye.

Devebhyah = for the sake of Devas, Brahma ha = supreme-Brahman verily, Vijigye = won (over Asuras).

‘Ha’ has a meaning of emphasis: Brahma ha = Brahman verily.

‘Ha’ also is an exclamation, suggesting surprise and joy, by remembering the unimaginable power of God.

Thus, the Asuras were defeated.

The God, within the Devas gave them the power to defeat the Asuras.

Staying within the Devas, He himself fought. Yes, He is within the Asuras also. He, himself made them defeated also.

Being within Devas, He won; being within Asuras, He lost!

What do you mean?

God didn’t win, neither did he lose.

He gave power to the Devas to win; and they won.

He withdrew the power of winning from Asuras; and they lost.

Both are puppets.

He verily is the puppeteer, toying with them both.

But this didn’t occur to the Devas.

What was the result?

*tasya ha brahmano vijaye deva amahiyanta*

Victory was of God, vanity was of the Devas.

Devas became arrogant, that they had won by their own might.

Vijaye = in the victory, Tasya Brahmanah = of that God, Devah = Deities, Amahiyanta = became vain, Ha = how strange; how amazing!

Even the wise Devas, forgetting the God inside, became vain, thinking that it was their own achievement! What then can be the state of the others!

This surprise is suggested by! Ha: tasya ha...

What happened as the result of this: ta aiksanta, asmakamevayam vijayosmakamevayam mahimeti.

‘This is very much our victory. The victory we gained with our own ability.

We are the sculptors of this victory. This is our own glory; our own greatness’!

Even Devas thought like this!

Ḍṛ = they, Aiksanta = thought that, Ayam = this, vijayah = victory, Asmakam Eva = is our’s only Ayam = this, Mahima = glory (is) Asmakam Eva = our’s only, Iti = like that.

Thus, pride and vanity enveloped the Devas also. They fancied that they were the real heroes, it was their own achievement!

In a sense, this is our own story.

Isn’ t it our state too?

Suppose, the task that we undertake succeeded easily. Then we too boast; with my tough guts I did it; did and succeeded; why should I care for others’?

Thus, when we win, we wear ourselves the headgear of victory. In case we lose?

The tone of our talk changes:

‘What to do sir. I tried very much. But God didn’t open His eye. I didn’t lose, God made me lose. Otherwise it is not possible at all that I did fail’!

When we win, it is not God who made us win; it is our victory. When we lose, it is not our failure; God made us fail!

The credit of failure is for God.

Failure also is not ours, neither the success. Behind the both, an unseen hand holds us and makes us move. But in the intoxication of victory, this matter is forgotten!

When even the Devas forgot, what about the state of others!

## Prose: two

What happened further?

*taddhaisam vijajnau, tebhyo ha pradurbabhuva,*

*tanna vyajanata kimidam yaksamiti \|\|2\|\|*

(God noticed the mistaken belief of these (Devas); to them (in Yaksa form) He appeared. (Devas) could not understand it, as to, what this yaksa was?)

God understood this weakness of the Devas: tat ha esam vijajnau.

Here the word ‘Tat’ is employed in two meanings.

‘Tat’ means, that which had come before.

Earlier both had come: The misconception of the Devas and Brah man principle.

Both of them are needed here in this sentence to complete it. Therefore, ‘Tat’ means, the wrong idea of Devas.

And ‘Tat’ means Brahman principle.

Tat = the misconception, Esam = of the Devas, Tat = that Brahman, Vijajnau = understood, Ha = how surprising!

Here too one. ‘Ha’ syllable has come: tat ha esam vijajnau.

Generally the commentators don’t search for the meaning in such words.

If it is verse they call it was line- fillers. If it is in prose, it is to deck the sentence, they say.

It is a style of Upanisad in language, they say.

Whatever others may say, we are after meaning.

The ancient Vedic-scholars firmly opine that, in the Vedic-scriptures there is not a single wasteful syllable or even a note, used just as a decoration.

There is a meaning even where it seems to be meaningless prima facie.

In the Stobhaksaras intruding syllables, used for singing of Samaveda, they had searched for meaning.

On the same line as theirs, let us proceed in our analysis.

There is a rare meaning here.

When it is said, ‘God noticed it’, a doubt crops up.

If it is said, ‘when Devas became proud, God understood’, it means, He didn’t know about it earlier.

Had He known about it earlier, He could have seen that they would not get proud. In that case, there would not have been a need to appear in the form of Yaksa and correct them.

As an answer to this doubt, the syllable ‘Ha’ has come here.

Isn’t it that He Himself has given vanity to the Devas? Then it becomes meaningless, that He didn’t know it before.

Being within them, He defeated Asuras; gave them the illusion that they themselves won. And He remained silent, until they flushed with pride.

Then, as if he came to know it afresh, He came as a Yaksa!

Oh! This ‘Ha’ syllable wakes us up telling ‘what a strange prank of God it is!’.

When all these things are there to be told, don’t you think the sentence would have been incomplete, if the ‘Ha’ was not there?

Who are those who have understood the infinite faces of His playfulness?

Seeing and relishing His games gives us an amazing, joyful feeling that is it: tat ha...

That Devas misconceived it, is a wonder, God came to know about it later, is another wonder of wonders: tat ha...

What did God do, when He came to know?

*tebhyo ha pradurbabhuva*

God appeared in front of them.

Aren’t they Aparoksa Jnanis - knowers of the God?

In the world of Devas, isn’t God always visible?

Then, ‘He appeared’ means what?

For that Sruti tells: tebhyam.

Tebhyah = for their sake, Pradurbabhuva = appeared, Ha = that too is a wonder.

He appeared not in front of them: He appeared for their sake. What does it mean?

For Devas, to remove their misconception, to remove their vanity, to give them the right awareness and redeem them, He appeared.

In the hearts of the Devas, in place of God, a vanity filled up. So, God vanished from the inside and appeared on the outside.

The devas too, who always see God, could not recognize, who it was!

He appeared in a form they would not recognize.

What a fascinating episode it is, where even the Devas fail to recognize God and ask Him ‘who are you? ’! For that reason comes the word ‘Ha’:

*tebhyo ha...*

As seen from the outside the Asuras failed, and Devas won.

But inside, Devas are failing; And Asuras are winning!

This is a more frightening defeat, than the outer defeat!

It is a psychological degradation!

Asura-power defeats the insights of Devas.

Devas wrongly understood; they flushed with vanity.

They got so much confused, that even when God Himself stood before them, they could not recognize Him!

What a plight, even to devas!

Thus, when the very power of purity goes off track, God had to come down to put it back on the right track: tebhyo ha...

In our meditations also we should remain very careful. We won’t know, at which moment, our mind losses the right track. At the end, even when God comes down, we may not recognize Him!

Remember, even to the Devas this happened: tebhyo ha...

The further statement tells the same:

*tanna vyajanata kimidam yaksamiti*

Here too, the word ‘Tat’, gives meaning in two levels:

*tat na vyajanata*

Tat = therefore, since Devas misconceived Him, Na vyajanata = Didn’t recognize, Tat = that Brahman.

Didn’t recognize as what?

*kimidam yaksamiti*

Idam = this, seen in front, Yaksam = mysterious form, Kim = what (is it?) iti = in this way.

If you say, God appeared in the form of Yaksa, how did He look like?

Yaksas, Gandharvas, all these belong to the retinue of Devas. Did God appear resembling them?

No, he didn’t look like the Yaksas, He appeared as Yaksa.

Again the thing became mysterious.

It is told like that, very much to make it mysterious.

‘Yaksa’ means venerable, precious, so enormous that it is beyond our understanding: yaksa pujayam.

On the whole, the form of Yaksa means, a mysterious form, which cannot be recognized as to what it is and who it is.

The word Yaksa is like the ‘X’ in English

A mysterious matter which we don’t understand, we call it ‘Yaksa Prasna’.

Similarly here also. God became a Yaksa, an ‘X’, even to the Devas. What is this?

Who had come? Why did He come? Thus He became a mysterious question.

He became an unbreakable riddle.

Nobody understood what it was. Yaksa - problem was not solved.

Devas gathered together under the leadership of Indra and discussed.

As the result of the discussion, it was decide that Agni be sent to Yaksa and to know who He was.

## Prose: three

The following portion of the chapter explains further development:

*te-agnimabruvan, jataveda etad vijanihi kimetad*

*yaksamiti, tatheti \|\|3\|\|*

(They told Agni: O, Jataveda, go, know It, about what is this Yaksa)

For any big job, the elders send the younger ones at first. If the younger ones don’t succeed, then the elders go. This is a common custom in the world.

In the hierarchy of the Devas, Agni is the youngest one.

There is a remark in Aitareyabrahmana:

*agnirvai devanam-avamah, vishnuh paramah tadantarena-anye devah*

(Amongst Devas, Agni is the lowest. Visnu is the highest. Between these two are the other Devas)

That is the reason, they send first Agni.

What did they say while sending him?

‘Oh, Agni, first you go. Comeback decoding Yaksa’s riddle’,

With this they sent him.

*te agnim-abruvan*

Te = the Devas, Abruvan = told, Agnim = the presiding deity of fire. What did they tell?

*jatavedah, etad vijanihi*

Jataveda = O, Agni, Etad = this, Vijanihi = (you) identify.

Identify what?

*kimetad yaksamiti*

Kim iti = who, what is, Etat = this, Yaksa, = mysterious person.

‘Jataveda’ is Agni’s another name depicting his quality.

Its meaning is very comely. It is praising Agni, encouraging him that he is competent at this job.

This praise is appropriate in this job of emissary.

‘Jataveda’ means one who knows everything in this creation.

*jatam-jatam vettiti jatadevah*

‘Jataveda’ means one who knows everything by birth: Sarvajna by birth: jatamatra eva sarvam vedeti jatadevah.

‘You are born knowledgeable. Therefore you are the right person to know this Yaksa. Therefore you go and get to know who it is.’

What is the reaction of Agni to it?

Is there anybody who won’t get charmed if told, ‘you are intelligent by birth’?

Agni too felt flattered by this praise: tatheti.

Tatha = let it be so, iti = thus (saying he agreed).

Agni thought, ‘among all, they chose me. Besides they called me ‘Jataveda’! I am the right one for this job’.

Pride increased; what should have vanished, it increased further.

‘Whoever it is I will face it; I definitely, will find the answer to this problem’, dreaming thus he went ahead.

## Prose: four

The forthcoming portion of prose tells about what happened later:

*tadabhyadravat, tamabhyavadat kositi, agnirva*

*ahamasmityabravit-jataveda va ahamasmiti \|\|4\|\|*

(Agni rushed towards Yaksa. Yaksa asked him, ‘who are you’. He said T am known as Agni; am famed as Jataveda’)

With the enthusiasm that he will find answer to the problem of the Devas, that he will find out about Yaksa before others do, Agni came rushing to Yaksa!

To say ‘he went’, Sruti employs the root ‘Dru’: abhi-adravat. Dru gatau.

‘Dravana’ means not a simple walk; it is ‘Drutagamana ’, going running, rushing.

The suggestiveness of this root is unique.

In Agni’s rush there is an itch of success, weight of vanity.

(Agni) Tat abhi = towards Yaksa, Adravat = came running.

‘Dravana’ has another meaning also: Melting, merging.

The suggestion of this meaning also is here. Agni melted away in front of Yaksa. To him it seemed as though, all his energy merged into the person of Yaksa.

Agni found it difficult to stand firm in front of Yaksa.

Yet he gathered courage and came to Yaksa in quick strides.

Agni ṣṁøṛ running; he saw Yaksa; came and stood close.

He remained standstill, forgetting the job he came for.

His words didn’t come out; the opened mouth remained open; the wide open eye remained as such.

Bewildered, words turning mute, body turning stiff Agni stood static. He, who had gone to speak to Yaksa, stood silent.

Instead, the Yaksa spoke to him!

*tamabhayavadat kositi*

(Yaksa) Abhi vadat = asked, Tam = agni, Kah asi iti = who are you?

Yaksa asked the question, which indeed was to be asked by Agni, ‘Hey, who are you?’

What did agni reply to this question:

*agnirva ahamasmityabravit, jatadeva va ahamasmiti*

Aham = I, Asmi = am, Agnih vai = well known as Agni, Iti abravit = so he said, Aham = I, Asmi = am, Jataveda Vai = famed as Jataveda, Iti (AbravIt) = so he said.

Again in this answer, Agni’s vanity is visible.

Agni says, ‘Do you know who I am? You must have heard about the deity famously known as Agni. I am that very Agni’.

He adds, ‘not only that, my fame too must have reached your ear. By birth I know everything. That is why I am called Jataveda. That title of honor is the proof of my greatness’.

Agni paraded his pride, boasted about his bigness. Even after seeing the Yaksa, he could not see his own smallness. Lamp bragged in front of Sun!

‘I am Agni. I am Jataveda’.

‘Vai’ is a particle, suggesting fame.

A mockery that ‘you haven’t heard of my fame? I am so famous!’ is suggested here: agnirvai...jatadeva vai...

We have seen what is ‘Jatavedas’. Now what is Agni’?

Agni is the one who burns down and swallows everything: attiti agnih.

The one, who is before everyone, is Agni: agre niyate iti agnih,

The one who is first among all, is Agni. Agryatvat-agnih.

The one who controls everything before others do is Agni: agre nayati niyamayati iti agnih.

Agni is the one who gives movement to inert things, which can’t move on their own: agam nayati iti agnih.

The controller of our body organs is Agni: angam nayati iti agnih.

Agni thought, T control everything, am the propeller of all; I can burn down everything; I know everything. That is why I am named Agni. It is not just a name. An appropriate name! I am also Jataveda. Rgveda emerged from me: agneh-rgvedah.. That is why I am Jataveda.

And so on, Agni put on airs about himself with vanity.

## Prose: five

The next prose section is the continuation of the conversation:

*tasminstvayi kim viryamiti,*

*apidam sarvam daheyam yadidam prthivyamiti \|\|5\|\|*

(Yaksa asked ‘what kind of power have you?’. Agni replied ‘whatever is there in this universe, all that I can burn it down’)

Yaksa, watching patiently the bragging of Agni, with equal patience had questioned: tasmin tvayi kim viryam iti.

‘Tasmin’ means in such a person.

Such person means, what kind of person?

The person who can burn down anything, who can control everything!

For that reason who is named Agni, famed as Jataveda! Therefore Yaksa said: tasmin tvayi.

‘In you, who is boasting this way! What kind of power have you? What are you capable of doing?’: kim viryam?

The word ‘Iti’ suggests the end of each conversation.

In the ancient systems, the word ‘iti’ does the function of inverted comma.

Using western punctuation system, if we have to write, tasminstvayi kim viryamiti, it will look like this: ‘tasminstvayi kim viryam’ iti.

Tasmin Tvai = in such (a person like) you, Kim = what, Viryam = capacity is there it = thus (asked the yaksa)

To that Agni rashly replies: apidam sarvam daheyam...

‘I can burn all this down’!

All this means what all? Yadidam prthivyam...

Whatever is there on this Prthivi, planet.

Prthivi means not just this earth.

Prthivi means, Bhuh, Bhuvah, Svah - earth, sky and heaven. Fire is everywhere.

Fire burns things not just on earth. It can burn everything everywhere.

Prthivi means the whole universe, the planetary system.

The material world spread everywhere: prathanat prthivi.

Or, Prthivi is Prakrti - Nature.

‘Anything created by nature, I am capable of burning it’ said Agni.

In reality, this conversation took place in the heaven itself, not on earth.

Even in the abode of the gods, there is a ground to stand upon. They too speak on that ground. That is the subtle Prthivi.

(Agni told) Iti = that, Yat idam = whatever is there, Prithivyam = in the earth, in Nature, Idam Sarvamapi = all that, Daheyam = I can burn down.

While Agni says he can burn down anything, from his mouth, the word ‘Prithivyam’ comes out, unawares.

There is appropriateness in it: What is the world? It is a collective of five elements. Earth, water, fire, air and space. These are the only things all over the universe. Fertilizer is mud, water, and fire. Rose plant also is mud, water and fire. The flower rose too. Fir^can’t burn space. It can’t burn air. Can fire burn fire? Fire can’t burn water; poured water extinguishes fire. Therefore, fire burns only the derivative of earth.

Therefore there is an element of truth in what Agni said: yadidam prthivyam.

## Prose: six

What was the reaction of the Yaksa to agni’s boasting?

The following prose section explains the further development:

*tasmei trnam nidadhavetad daheti, tadupapreyaya sarvajavena, tanna sasaka dadghum, sa tata eva nivavrte neitadasakam vunatum yadetad yaksameti \|\|6\|\|*

(It (Yaksa) kept a leaf of grass in front of Agni, saying ‘burn this’.

With all his speed Agni pounced on it. But he didn’t succeed burning it. With that he returned, and reported it to the Devas, ‘I can’t understand; what mystery it is!’.)

God, in the form of Yaksa, thought: I should break Agni’s horns of pride; vanquish his vanity.

Thinking thus, Yaksa placed a grass in front of Agni:

*tasmei trnam nidadhau*

And he said, ‘Unless I see with my own eyes, how can I trust your words? Keep aside the talk of burning the whole universe. If you are capable, burn this piece of grass, let us see’: etad daha iti.

Tasmei = to Agni, to test Agni’s power, to break Agni’s pride (Yaksa) Nidadhau = Kept in front, Trnam = a leaf of grass (and told) Iti = thus, Etat Daha = burn this.

What did Agni do then?

Already he was melting. Melting he perspired. He had lost this confidence.

Yet without showing it, he pounced on the grass. Jumped, with all his power of burning the universe: tadupapreyaya sarvajavena.

‘Java’ means, speed, it also means capacity:

(Agni) Sarvajavena = applying all his power, Upa + Preyaya = pounced upon, Tat = on the leaf of grass.

Yet the grass didn't burnt at all: tanna sasaka dagdhum.

(Agni) Na Sasaka = didn’t succeed, Dagdhum = to burn, Tat = the grass.

At least after this Agni should have realized his littleness.

He should have realized that the Indweller within him, who staying within, has given him the power to burn, He verily is in front of him.

At least now he should have bowed down his head and asked: ‘Sir, who are you’?

But he didn’t ask at all. He, who came with vanity got more humiliated!

Without words, he returned silent: sa tat eva nivavrte.

Sah = Agni, Tatah eva = with that only, Ni + Vavrte= returned!

The expected job was not done at all. With head hung down he came back, humiliated.

And Devas were waiting for his report!

Agni’s answer to their query was disappointing:

*neitadasakam vunatum yadetad yaksamitti!*

‘What is that Yaksa, how it is and all that, 1 failed to understand’ admitted Agni.

Na asakam = I was not able to, Vijnatum = to understand, Etad = this riddle, Etat Yaksam = this yaksa, Yat = who it is, Iti = (Agni said) so.

## Prose: seven

Vayu is senior to Agni. The next turn is of Vayu.

Agni is the deity of mouth. Vayu is the deity of nose.

We speak because of Agni. We breathe because of Vayu.

Thus, in the life world, Vayu is above Agni.

In the matter-world also: Agni is the Deva of earth region. Vayu is the Deva of space region.

Now, after Agni, it is the turn of Vayu.

Devas entrusted to Vayu the job which Agni could not do:

*atha vayumabruvan, vayavetad vijnanihi kimetad yaksamiti,*

*tatheti \|\|7\|\|*

Then (the Devas) told Vayu: ‘Oh, Vayu, go and get to know, what is this Yaksa’. Vayu said ‘Yes’.)

Who is this Vayu? There is one chief - Vayu.

He comes in the third grade of the hierarchy; he is equal to Caturmukha who preaches this Upanisat to Siva.

After it, there are 49 Vayus. These collectively are called Marudgana.

Amongst these, one is Aham - Prana. He belongs to 9'hgrade and is only one step below Indra.

Another is Pravaha Vayu, of 11th grade. He is also called Bhuta Vayu, and Nasikya Vayu.

He is 4 steps above Agni, who is in 15thgrade.

The rest 47, belong to 18thgrade. They are 3 steps below Agni.

Thus there are so many Vayus.

Now, which one is this Vayu, that Devas sent to the Yaksa?

It can’t be any of the 47, who are below Agni.

Where Agni had failed, these would not have been sent, since they are below Agni.

It is evident that the Vayu referred now is above the grade orbit of Agni.

In that sphere there are three: Pravaha Vayu, Aham Pranavayu and the Main Vayu.

The question of main Vayu doesn’t arise, since he is much above Indra.

This Upanisat tells that, even when Vayu could not succeed, finally Indra was sent.

This much is clear from this story:

This Vayu is above Agni of 15thgrade and below from Indra of 8thgrade.

Two such ones are: Aham Prana of 9thgrade and Pravaha Vayu of 11thgrade.

Of these two, who did go to see the Yaksa?

It should be decided in the total context of the earlier and later parts of this text.

In the previous chapter, there was a reference about sense-organs and their respective Deities.

Agni is the presiding Deity of speech organ. The next one was about Nasikya Vayu, who is the Deity of breathing.

Agni is the Deity of earth. The next episode is about Bhuta Vayu, Deity of sky.

Finally Indra comes, the Deity of heaven.

Therefore the Vayu referred here is, Nasikya Vayu of Pindanda - microcosmic world and Bhuta Vayu of Brahmanda - macrocosmic world.

Verily he is Pravaha Vayu of 11thgrade.

According to some, though Aham Prana of 9thgrade also is Nasikya Vayu, he is not Bhuta Vayu. Therefore Pravaha is the right Vayu, since he is applicable to both Pindanda & Brahmanda.

So, it is evident, that Pravaha Vayu is the one, who was sent by the Devas to the Yaksa, after Agni’s failure.

*atha vayumabruvan…*

Atha = after the return of Agni, (Devas) Vayum = to Pravaha Vayu, Abruvan = told.

The job which Agni could not complete, Pravaha Vayu may do it, since he is more qualified than Agni. With this hope Devas decided to send him.

Devas told Pravaha, as they did to Agni also: ‘Oh, Pravaha, you are senior to Agni; you know more. At least you get to know Yaksa’.

*vayo etad vijanihi kimetad yaksamiti*

Vayo = Î Vayu, Vijanihi = get to know, Etad = this, Iti = as to, Kim= what is, Etat = this, Yaksam = Yaksa.

Vayu thought that he will succeed where Agni failed. He decided not to lose this opportunity of pride.

Therefore, he immediately agreed to the request of the Devas: tatheti.

(Vayu said) ‘Tatha Iti = let it be so’.

## Prose: eight

The further prose portion tells that the same story of Agni was repeated, when Vayu too went to see the Yaksa;

*tadabhyadravat, tamabhyavadat kositi, vayurva ahamasmityabravit,*

*matarisva va ahamasmlti \|\|8\|\|*

(Vayu rushed towards it. Yaksa asked him, who are you? Vayu replied, ‘I am known as Vayu; am famed as Matarisva’.)

It is true that Vayu’s status is higher than Agni.

But the situation didn’t improve by his visit.

He came and saw Yaksa awe struck, remained open-mouthed and got wide-eyed.

He couldn’t speak at all.

Yaksa itself spoke, ‘who are you’?

Tat = towards the Yaksa (Vayu), Abhi+Adravat = rushed. (Yaksa) Abhi+avadat = asked, Tam = him, Kah asi iti = that who are you.

Like Agni, Vayu too gave a childish answer: vayurvai … matarisva vai...

‘You don’t know who am I? I am Vayu. All know it. Not only that, I am famous as Matarisva! That is an honorary title for my greatness. That too you don’t know’.

Thus Pravaha Vayu too boasted about himself.

What is Vayu? What is Matarisva? What are the definitions of these words?

One who can move everywhere is Vayu: vati iti vayuh, va gatau.

One who reaches out the smell of the earth to nose, is Vayu. That is why he is named as ‘Gandhavaha’. Vati iti vayuh, va gandhane.

One who upholds and sustains the planetary systems is Vayu: vayate iti vayuh; vaya bandhane.

The one who moves around outside as air, and as breath inside, gives life to the living-world is Vayu: vati sarvatra gacchati; ayuhpradasceti vayuh.

Pravaha said, T move everywhere. There is no place that I have not seen; no spot I haven’t touched.

Nose inhales smell because of me. I make the nose also breathe. The power that carries the entire universe, is me. The stars and planets are knit in the empty space, because of me. The mankind breathes and lives because of me. What, you don’t know that!?’:

‘Not just that: matarisva va ahamasmi.

My fame is as ‘Matarisva’.

What does it mean?

‘Mata’ means Sky. Creator of air. Akasad vayuh.

Vayu’s father is Akasa-ether. From ether Vayu was born.

Here ‘Mata’ doesn’t mean ‘mother’; it is ‘father’.

Therefore, this word ‘Mata’ is not in feminine gender; it is in masculine gender.

One who can move about, in ether is 'Matarisva ’.

*matari svayatiti matarisva, svi gatau*

He who rising as storm in the sky and plucks and flings out plants and trees is Matarisva.

*matari svayati vardhata iti matarisva; svi vrddhau*

Pravaha brags about his fame again: ‘I am not the one who just walks on earth. Am not just stuck in earth like Agni. My place is in sky. I fly around sky. Not only that. Turning into a typhoon I can yank anything and fling it out. That is the reason all call me ‘Matarisva out of respect. You don’t know this?!’.

It doesn’t end here. ‘Matr’ means speech, Vedic words. One who moves in it is Matarisva. ‘Agni is Jataveda. But I am Matarisva. The big scholar who can easily move around in the Vedas!’.

Aham = I, Asmi=am, Vayuh Vei=am famous as Vayu, Iti = so, AbravIt = (Vayu) said, Aham = I, Asmi = am, Matarisva Vei = a famous as Matarisva Iti = thus, (Vayu) Abravit = said.

## Prose: nine

May be Vayu is above Agni.

But his pride also is bigger than Agni.

If the big ones stumble, it will be a big fall.

The following sentence tells that the reaction of the Yaksa was as composed as it was earlier:

*tasminstvayi kim viryamiti, apidam sarvamadadiya yadidam prthivyamiti \|\|9\|\|*

The same usual question. The same usual answer:

Tasmin = in such, Tvayi = you, Kim = what, Viryam = capacity is there? Iti = so, (asked the Yaksa).

Prthivyam = in this universe, Yat idam = whatever is there, Idam sarvam = all that, Adadiya = I can pluck and throw, Iti = so (said Vayu).

## Prose: ten

‘Is it so? Then pluck and fling this leaf of grass, let us see’, saying this the Yaksa placed the grass in front of Vayu.

Earlier Agni underwent the ‘Fire-Test’. Now it is the ‘fire-Test’ for Vayu.

*tasmai trnam nidadhavetadadatsveti, tadupa preyaya sarvajavena tanna sasakadatum, sa tata eva nivavrte naitadasakam vijnatum yadetad yaksamiti \|\|10\|\|*

(Yaksa placed a leaf of grass in front of Vayu, to test his capacity, telling ‘Fly it’. Gathering all his might, Vayu pounced on the grass. But he could not fly it. With that, Vayu returned saying, ‘1 cannot understand it; what this Yaksa is’.)

Yaksa must have laughed at Vayu’s plight.

Vayu too got mocked in front of Yaksa.

Yaksa had asked, ‘What can you do?’

Vayu became a swollen-headed bull; cackling cock.

‘If I turn into a storm, I can pluck and fling the tops of hills and jungles’.

Vayu sang the usual tune.

Yaksa spoke laughing, ‘No need to fling hills and jungles. If you are really capable, just fly this leaf of grass, let me see’.

*tasmai trnam nidadhau, etadadatsveti*

Tasmei = to pravaha Vayu, to test his power, to break his pride, (Yaksa) Ni+dadhau = placed in front, Trnam = a grass, A + datsva = fly, Etat = this, Iti = (telling) thus.

What happened later was as usual.

Repeating the previous sentences again, indicating that in front of Yaksa the plights of Agni and Vayu were same, Upanisat imparts a fresh sparkle to the language.

Agni and Vayu did the same, chewing the chewed.

The question, Who is Yaksa, remained a riddle.

Vayu also pounced on grass, with all his might. But the leaf of grass didn’t even bend.

*tadupapreyaya sarvajavena …*

(Vayu), Sarvajanena = with all might, Tat upa+preyaya = pounced on the grass, Na Sasaka = he was not able, Adatum = to lift, Tat = that.

Vayu’s pride too didn’t allow him to surrender and request the Yaksa.

Confused, he didn’t know what to do. Feeling crest-fallen, he too returned empty-handed: sa tata eva nivirte…

Sah = Vayu, Tatah eva = with that only, Ni+vavrte = returned; Na asakam = I failed, Vijnatum = to understand, Etat = this, Yat = as to what is, Etat = this, Yaksam = yaksa, Iti = (saying) thus.

## Prose: eleven

Now, it is the turn of Indra.

Again all the Devas gathered and told Indra, ‘Now you yourself go and see’.

*athendramabruvan maghavannetad vijanihi kimetad yaksamiti, tatheti tadabhyadravat, tasmat tiro dadhe \|\|11\|\|*

(They (Devas) came to Indra and told him, ‘O Maghavan, go, get to know what is this Yaksa. He said alright and rushed towards it. It vanished from his sight.)

Devas called Indra as ‘Maghavan’.

What does it mean? ‘Magha’ means ‘Makha’, Yajna sacrifice.

The one principally worshipped in Yajna is ‘Maghavan’.

In Yajna, excepting Prana and Narayana, the main oblation is only to Indra.

Hence is he chief of the Devas.

Among the Deities related to the three Lokas, Earth, Sky and Heaven, Indra is the highest.

Agni is the Deity of Earth. Vayu of the Sky and Indra of Heaven. Therefore he is ‘Makhavan’; Makhavan becomes ‘Maghavan’.

*tam va etam makhavantam santam maghavanitya caksate paroksena*

‘Magha’ means wealth: dhanam vava magham.

‘Magha’ means, success: yaso vava magham.

Maghavan means rich one. The master of the riches of the three worlds.

‘Magha’ also means, wealth of knowledge.

Maghavan means, a person rich with knowledge.

He is a greater Jnani than Agni, and greater than Vayu also.

His fame is glorified in all the three worlds.

By that too, he is Maghavan.

Remembering all this greatness of Indra, Devas called him: maghavan.

‘You are such a Maghavan superior to all of us, please, you go yourself, get to know the Yaksa’. Thus Devas requested him: athendramabruvan...

Atha = then, (Devas) Abruvan = told, lndram = Indra: Maghavan = O Lord of Yajnas, Î Lord of the riches of the three worlds, Î great scholar, Î famed great one, Vijanihi = get to know, Etat = it, Kim iti = what is, Etat = this, Yaksam = the mysterious one, Iti = saying (thus).

Indra too agreed to this request. He too proceeded there:

*tatheti tadabhyadravat*

(Indra) Tatha = let it be so, Iti Adravat = rushed, Tat abhi = towards Yaksa.

But! The Yaksa was not there!

Earlier, Agni and Vayu had gone there, had seen Yaksa. But had returned without getting to know him.

Now Indra went. Went, firmly determined to know it. But the Yaksa had vanished. Tasmat torodadhe.

(The Yaksa) Tirodadhe = vanished, Tasmat = from Indra’s sight, or, from the earlier site.

The Yaksa which showed itself to the two earlier, why did it vanish from Indra? What was behind it?

God, who came in the form of Yaksa knows Indra’s capability.

This Indra won’t return empty-handed like the previous ones. He is clever enough to complete his job.

He can be tested with the question, ‘What is your capacity?’. By that, he won’t come back without answering. He will ask back, ‘Who are you that made me also powerless?’.

Well, if he asks, there may be answers.

But there is a system here.

About God, only through Guru, knowledge should be imparted.

The direct Guru of Indra is Parvati.

While Indra is in 8thgrade, Parvati is in 7th.

Therefore, only through Parvati, Indra should be initiated.

She is not only the direct Guru of Indra. She is the Deity of speech also.

With this incident, the importance of the Guru tradition are we informed.

*mukundabhaktyai gurubhakti-jayai.*

*yasya deve para bhaktir-yatha deve tatha gurau.*

For all these reasons, Yaksa-God, vanished.

## Prose: twelve

Yaksa vanished in Akasa - the sky; and she, Parvati appeared:

*sa tasminnevakase striyamajagama bahu sobha manam-umam haimavatim, tam hovaca kimetad yaksamiti \|\|12\|\|*

(God, in Yaksa form, made a woman appear in the same sky, from which He had vanished. The woman, appeared twinkling with glow; the woman, Uma, daughter of Himavan. Confused Indra asked her in surprise, ‘What is this Yaksa?’.

In the beginning of this sentence there is a word: sah.

‘Sah’ means, ‘He’; the one referred earlier.

He means who?

Earlier there was a reference about two: One is, God in the form of Yaksa, and the other, Indra, who comes to see Him.

In fact, reference to God is in neuter gender, ‘Yaksam’.

Later, in the answer also the word is in neuter gender, ‘Brahma’.

Therefore, a doubt may crop up, as to how the reference to God comes with the word ‘Sah’, which is in masculine gender.

For that, we have to go to the beginning of the Upanisat.

There, the first reference to God is very much in masculine gender: ka u devah.

Therefore, here also, ‘Sah’ means, that God in Yaksa form: sa tasminnevakase...

Sah = that God in Yaksa form, Tasmin eva = in the same, Akase = sky, where He had appeared, Ajagama = brought, Striyam = woman.

As a matter of fact, ‘Ajagama’ means ‘he came’, not ‘he brought’.

‘Agamayamasa’ would mean ‘he brought’.

To express freedom, going for such usages is a Vedic custom.

In everyday talk also we can see such usages. One constructs a house. He shows the construction to his friends and says, ‘I built this house’.

‘1 built it’ means, ‘I got it built’. The carpenters and masons are the ones who physically had built it.

However, only the person speaking has the freedom, and the possession of the house. Therefore he tells with pride, T built this house’.

Similarly, here also, Parvati comes, but not due to her wishes; she comes by command of God; to follow His command.

God didn’t come directly. He sent Parvati.

Parvati came. God also came, residing within Parvati.

The commentary of Traivikramaryadasa also supports this:

*ajagama agamayamasa*

Or, ‘Sah’ means Indra.

Sah = that Indra, Tasminneva-akase = in the same sky where Yaksa was seen, Ajagama = came across, Striyam = a woman.

A woman was seen in the place of Yaksa.

She was not a stranger. Indra identified her: ‘Oh! She is Parvati’:

*bahu sobhamanam-umam haimavatim*

(Ajagama = he came across, Bahu = very, Sobhamanam = lovely and lustrous, Umam = Uma, Heimavatim = daughter of Himavan Deity of the Himalayas.)

Here, Parvati is called ‘Uma’.

It is an appropriate name to the Divinity of speech, of knowledge.

‘U’ = excellent, regarding excellent God, ‘Ma’ = knowledge.

There is another interesting thing here. With a little change ‘aum’ becomes ‘uma’.

The first syllable of aum that is A', if it comes in the end, the word becomes ‘uma': U+M+A = UMA.

It is the right name for the goddess of speech, who came to preach Indra about God, who is addressed by aum.

‘u’ means Ugra, the fearful Siva.

‘Ma’ means, wealth, riches.

‘Uma’ is Sivas wealth; his life partner.

Uma has two adjectives: bahu sobhamanam and haimavatim..

‘Bahusobhamana’ means, very beautiful or lustrous.

Which beauty can match the beauty of the speech?

Which charm can match the charm of knowledge?

Which lustre can match the lustre of wisdom?

*“vagbhusanam bhusanam”*

Which greater charming lady can be there than the goddess of wisdom?

Such glow of spiritual wisdom was brimming on Parvati’s face: bahu sobhamanam.

This adjective suggests that she is the right teacher for Indra.

There is a saying in the Sastras: yatra rupam tatra gunah.

Rupa - looks, and Guna - fine qualities, are related!

Where there is real beauty, there will be right qualities too. Parvati is very pretty, and that, she is a mine of fine qualities also, like knowledge and bliss: bahu sobhamanam.

‘Haimavati’ can be explained in two ways.

Himavan’s daughter is ‘Haimavati’.

‘Hema’ is gold.

‘Haima’ is golden ornament.

One who wears a lot of fine gold-ornaments is ‘Heimavati’.

Right from the beginning Parvati is an embodiment of beauty. Added to it are fine golden jewels. She doesn’t need gold to be beautiful. It is that ornament gains beauty by her wearing it.

Any amount of jewelry can’t be make an ugly lady beautiful.

Therefore this adjective also gives a special stress to Parvati’s very rare beauty.

‘Himavan’s daughter,’ is also the intended meaning here.

Siva’s wife has two incarnations:

First, as ‘Sati’, daughter of Daksa Prajapati. Secondly as ‘Gauri’, daughter of Himavan. ‘Uma’ is a special name she gets, when she takes birth as daughter of Himavan.

Whom Indra saw was not Sati, in the form of Daksa’s daughter, but Parvati in the-form of Himavan’s daughter.

This adjective comes here to suggest this special matter also.

‘Haima’ is a name of Siva also.

Now there comes a new dimension to the adjective.

‘Haimavati’ means one who has come along with Siva.

The testimony referred by Acarya Madhva says: ‘God did not come alone in the form of Yaksa. There were Siva and Parvati too along with Him.

And, Caturmukha too was there.

*uma-siva-samanvitah, brahmana capi sahitah*

When God vanished, Brahma and Siva too vanished along with Him.

Only Uma remained and became visible.

To suggest this very rare matter too, the adjective ‘Haimavati’ is employed here.

The moment Indra sees Uma, he recognizes her: ‘This is the goddess of my worship. The deity of speech. My mentor; my mother'.

That is how he asks her confidently, ‘who was it, that appeared in Yaksa form?’

tam hovaca, kimetad yaksamiti

Iti = thus(Indra) Uvaca = asked, Tam = Her, Ha = with joy and wonder, Kim = who is, Etat = this, Yaksam = mysterious being?

The Yaksa being visible, when Agni and Vayu came; it becoming invisible when he came; and unexpectedly coming across Parvati in the same place, these things surprised Indra.

His mentor, his mother has appeared, now his problems will be solved. This is a happy thing.

To suggest Indra’s overwhelming sentiments, this ‘Ha’ has come. tam hovaca.

Therefore he asked with courage and confidence, ‘who is this mysterious being’: kimetad yaksamiti.

It is a strange episode.

It is a story told by Brahma to Siva.

In this story also, indirectly there is a reference about Brahma and Siva. And directly about Parvati, wife of Siva.

It is very much a story of Siva and Parvati. Told, to Siva himself! What is this?

It may look absurd outwardly.

But within it there is sense.

Brahma preached a Tattva to Siva. As a proof of that Tattva, he told Siva, Siva’s own story.

‘What I had preached you, the same thing, your wife Parvati taught Indra! You too were its witness, weren’t you?’

Thus, Brahma uses the same ancient experience of Siva, as proof of his preaching to Siva now.

This is one face. There is another face also:

This was the Mantra seen by Caturmukha Brahma and Pancamukha Siva, for the enlightenment of mankind. Not just for themselves.

They saw this Mantra, for the deliverance of the worshippers. Sivas questioning was also for our sake.

Brahma replied also for our sake.

At the same time, From Brahma’s words, Siva’s awareness got further consolidated.

Thus, with the curious question of Indra, the third chapter of this Upanisat ends.

## Section: Four

## Prose: one

With Parvati’s reply, the fourth chapter, the last one begins:

*brahmeti hovaca, brahmano va etadvijayemah tyadhvamiti.*

*tato haiva vidancakara brahmeti \|\|1\|\|*

(Parvati said, ‘It is Brahman, Brahma Tattva won and you prided that you have won!’ Thus Indra understood ‘that Yaksa verily is Brahman’.)

Parvati told him ‘You thought you defeated those Asuras. Do you know who defeated them, residing within you? He verily is Para Brahman:

*brahmeti hovaca*

Parvati said: ‘Do you know who appeared before you in Yaksa form, who disappeared when you came down? He verily is Para-Brahman.

*brahmeti hovaca*

Uvaca (she) = said, Brahma iti = (that Yaksa) verily is Brahman, Ha = Aha!

In this sentence also ‘Ha’ is there, suggesting surprise.

Aha! What a surprise! Even the wise Devas too forgot that the God pervading inside, does everything!

If even the Devas can be deceived by delusion, what about the lot of poor others! How incredible is God’s Maya!

Well, at last Indra could get to know about It. And through him, the illusion of the Devas will be cleared out. Aha! That is a happy outcome. That is the suggestion of ‘Ha’.

Parvati clarifies her intention:

*brahmano va etadvijaye amahiyadhvamiti*

‘Etad + vijaya’ means, the success over against the Devils; it is not your winning. It is the success of the God within you: brahmanah.

*namah karta harih karta*

*na rte tvat kriyate kincanare*

Thus, God being the prime cause of all happenings is well known in all the Gastric texts.

*brahmano vai*

You didn’t win, He made you win.

By His blessing you succeeded against the Devils:

*brahmanah prasadat etesam vijaye*

You forget the Pervader within you. You turned proud that you won yourself: amahiyadhvam.

‘Amahiyadhvam’ is the past form of the root ‘Maha pujayam’. ‘You thought that the credit of this success belongs to us, this is very much the greatness of our valor’, this is the total sense of this word.

Brahmanah by kindness of God, Etadvijaye = in this victory over devils, Amahiyadhvam = you became proud, Iti = so (said Parvati).

Indra realized his folly by Parvati’s words:

*tato haiva vidancakara brahmeti*

(By that talk Indra realized that it was Brahman.)

Indra realized that it was that ‘Paramapurusa - the great God, who had vanished when he came, who, though stood visible to Agni and Vayu, remained mysterious without opening out Himself, He Who had given them power to defeat the Asuras, staying inside the Devas, gave them the credit for the victory, now kindly gave the right awareness through Parvati. It was none else, it was He, the God.’

Among the three who went to see the Yaksa, it was Indra who understood it first. The others didn’t ‘see’ It though they saw It.

Indra ‘saw’ It without seeing It.

He knew It not by himself, but only through the preaching of Parvati:

*tato ha eva*

He got a right Guru, The student understood God without much difficulty. The immense joy that Indra gained from this experience of God, Upanisat expresses through ‘Ha’ and makes us too taste it: tato ha...

Tatah eva = from Parvati’s preaching only, Vidamcakara (Indra) = understood, Brahma iti = that it is Brahman Ha = what a joy!

## Prose: two

All these Devas are not uniform.

There is a hierarchy amongst them.

Some are senior, some are juniors.

Their seniority is according to their awareness of God.

Those who understood first, are the seniors; those corning to know later are the juniors.

The ones who understood more are the elders; those who understood less are the younger ones.

The following section comes to present the same message

*tasmad va ete deva atitaramivanyan devan yadgnirvayurindrah, te hyenannedisiham pasprsu ste hyenat prathamo vidancakara bhahmeti \|\|2\|\|*

(By this itself, these Devas became greater than the other Devas; these Agni, Vayu and Indra. Didn’t these experience Brahmatattva from close? Weren’t they the first to understand it as Brahman?)

Agni was the first to go to understand the Yaksa.

Though he failed, he was the first one who tried to know it. Afterwards, Vayu went to know It.

Though he too failed, he didn’t hesitate in making the effort to know.

Finally Indra went and returned after coming to know It.

Subsequently, Vayu understood it through Indra. Later, Agni also.

Therefore these three became more important than the others.

Tasmat vei = by this only, Agnih, Vayuh, Indrah iti yat = these Agni, Vayu and Indra, Ete Devah = these Devas, Atitaram iva = excelled.

Here ‘vai’ comes in two meanings: tasmad vai.

One is about emphasis the other is about fame.

Therefore, only because they knew before others, these three became more important than the other Devas.

*tasmad vai*

It is a very well renowned fact in all spiritual texts, that these three are above the other Devas. Tasmad vai atitaramiva.

This ‘Atitaramiva’ is another strange usage.

Atitaram means ‘excelling’.

‘Atitaram iva’ means, those who seem to be excelling others; who seem to be great.

If it is said, ‘seem to be great’, it means that in reality they aren’t great.

Here, it needs to be told that these three are really greater than the others.

Then why this expression, ‘seem to be’, why this word ‘Iva’? There is a special meaning for it, in the Vedic-scripture.

The word ‘Iva’ tells about two contradictory aspects.

Let us take a common example: ‘He seems to be a culprit’.

What does it mean?

It means, he is a culprit; and not a culprit also!

From one angle, he is a culprit.

From another angle, he is innocent.

Similarly here also: It seems, Agni, Vayu and Indra seem to be greater than others.

It means: yes, they are greater; and no, they are not!

From one angle they are greater and from another angle, they are not.
